Research program · Earlier searched induction proof · Finite-inventory pruning lemma

Left-successor addition

ResultComplete grammarStructural pruningForward controlControl tile rowsPrimitive certificateCosts and unknownsEvidence

Eight bounded GCTS searches remain unknown; ordinary forward control produces a candidate whose native whole check reaches the heap limit. Every recorded point tree and native outcome, including unknowns, passed independent reconstruction/replay.

\(\forall\,\mathit{x}\;\left(\forall\,\mathit{y}\;\left(\left(\operatorname{S}\!\left(\mathit{x}\right)+\mathit{y}\right)=\operatorname{S}\!\left(\left(\mathit{x}+\mathit{y}\right)\right)\right)\right)\)

In words: adding to a successor on the left gives the successor of the corresponding sum. The exact input theory is unchanged from the earlier zero-left experiment: \(\forall\,\mathit{x}\;\left(\left(\mathit{x}+0\right)=\mathit{x}\right)\) and \(\forall\,\mathit{x}\;\left(\forall\,\mathit{y}\;\left(\left(\mathit{x}+\operatorname{S}\!\left(\mathit{y}\right)\right)=\operatorname{S}\!\left(\left(\mathit{x}+\mathit{y}\right)\right)\right)\right)\), with the natural-number induction schema enabled. The target is absent from the axioms. This is a recursive-addition fragment, not all Peano arithmetic.

159complete five-node terms
39 / 48retained inference interfaces
24fully native-admitted union definitions
0searched candidate observations

The entire declared grammar is preserved

Generic infrastructure enumerates every term through five syntax nodes, every recursive-equation direction, expression occurrence and missing-variable substitution; both target-variable induction alternatives; explicit conditional step contexts; and every contextual generalization. No target-proof sequence is supplied. The zero-left lane adds the actual previously searched, completely native-checked theorem as a closed reusable proof, never a new axiom. Its one-edge applications have two explicit inputs: the current equality and the proved universal theorem. The authored local interfaces are not called learned.

InventoryTermsFormula basisFull interfacesFull local definitionsExposed instantiationsExposed induction instances
without-transfer159206720978222022482
with-searched-zero-left1592068254812732024072

The two complete primitive diagnostic streams total 384,403,080 uncompressed bytes. Every instance was independently reconstructed. These streams are exposed preparation catalogs, remain natively unadmitted, and are not the searched contextual grammar. There are no admissible exposed quantifier-distribution instances because both declared variables occur in each relevant antecedent; guarded distribution does occur inside the fully checked generic induction interfaces.

Proved structural pruning before admission

All full-grammar templates, including native-unresolved ones, enter a least fixed point from every zero-input output. A rule is removed only when at least one premise is outside that closed set. Induction over strict-prior proof positions proves that no finite full-grammar proof word can contain such a rule. Conditional assumptions remain explicit inside implication formulas. The certificate stores every full edge, every formula rank/layer and each removed rule’s unreachable premise; an independent priority worklist reconstructs them all.

This is authored graph analysis, not a learned failure marking. It preserves all successful finite proof words in this grammar and makes no claim about unrestricted first-order nonprovability. It also computes bounded syntactic derivability; GCTS subsequently assembles actual placements and source-explicit proof words. It selects no target-proof path or preferred induction variable.

without-transfer2097 → 39822 → 17327209448 → 3590
with-searched-zero-left2548 → 481273 → 24377278770 → 4628

All 17 no-transfer definitions are byte-identical members of the 24-definition union. Only this complete retained union is admitted natively; the full unpruned catalogs remain available. Read the safety lemma and scope.

What the automatic point search did

Four fresh models use the frozen complete frontier graph: global dead ends, then global forced moves, then earliest generation, with complete fallback and exact rollback. Every required C/G/I1/I2 point starts at generation zero; occupancy and extended scalar catalog markings are exact, with fixed orientation. Every retained interface and strict-prior source alternative is generated. Two repetitions per inventory preserve the same inventory; the without/with-transfer inventories differ semantically and do not provide an equal-inventory search-speed comparison. No RL policy was trained.

Necessary rank projection: smaller graph, same early search

For zero-based row \(j\) a placed output must have minimum complete-hypergraph derivation rank at most \(j\). Each selected strict-prior source row must likewise be at least the required premise rank. Every full retained candidate was classified through the complete shared graph. All eligible keys remain; no target backcone or selected path is used. Independent full-universe reconstruction and a strict-prior safety proof preserve exactly the same successful finite word language.

All four remain search-budget unknowns. All 20,004 new nodes were independently replayed. The original and projected runs have identical early tree decisions/counts within each inventory at this attempt boundary; the representation reduction has not demonstrated better search ordering.

A matched ordinary forward-chaining control

After all eight GCTS unknowns were preserved and audited, a separately frozen ordinary forward algorithm traversed every native-admitted retained hyperedge to the complete fixed point. It kept the first ready edge in unchanged catalog order for each newly reachable formula, then reconstructed a strictly ordered proof DAG. No human derivation, chosen induction variable or target path was provided. This algorithm is a control, not GCTS, a GCTS proposal, or RL.

InventoryAll hyperedgesReached formulasEdge scansExtraction secondsWord rowsFits 12 rows
without-transfer39323120.02811True
with-searched-zero-left48373840.03111True

Both lanes produce byte-identical 11-row requests. The zero-left inventory theorem is absent from their target dependency. This is concrete evidence that the declared finite grammar can produce a target candidate while blind GCTS struggles with ordering at the recorded budget. It gives no inventory benefit here and no RL advantage. The point encoding below also independently verifies an exact 12-row embedding with one irrelevant zero-input filler.

Whole native certification remains unknown. The 11-row request and its mechanically expanded 79-command / shared 67-command primitive version both reach unchanged heap band 26, head 73,178 equal to capacity 73,178. Independent complete parser input/output/step/FNV replay reproduces both limits. Local admission and host logical acceptance do not replace unfinished whole native checking.

The second inventory uses the exact first-inventory native request results, with those historical costs disclosed rather than charged twice.

Whole checking of the automatically selected certificate

Each complete point trace is checkpointed before validation. The original selected request contains all actually used local definitions. A generic expansion maps those local assumptions to their actual earlier sources, then exact-formula DAG sharing and dependency slicing produce a primitive certificate. Both versions retain the byte-identical original theory and target and add no theorem-specific inference. Exact repeat validation requests may reuse an earlier completed native result; historical time is shown below, and fresh verification cost is counted only once.

Actual selected tile rows, formulas and words

Purple C records the command; gold G its admitted interface; green I1 and orange I2 connect to actual earlier source formulas. Dashed gray assigns an explicit unused value. These are high-level point tiles with extended markings, not a claim that a primitive square Wang occupancy search ran. Long formulas have horizontal scrolling. Expand a row for the native-checked local derivation and exact point values.

GCTS supplied no selected proof under these budgets. The following 11-row word was automatically extracted by the separate ordinary forward control. Its exact point tiles and extended scalar markings passed independent full-model/tiling checks, but its whole native certificate remains a heap-budget unknown. It is not a GCTS discovery.

  3. \(\left(\operatorname{S}\!\left(\mathit{x}\right)+0\right)=\operatorname{S}\!\left(\left(\mathit{x}+0\right)\right)\)

    Use one recursive addition equation in one expression context. The chosen edge uses axiom AZ. Inputs: line 2.

    Expand the native-checked local derivation (15 commands)
    1. \(\left(\operatorname{S}\!\left(\mathit{x}\right)+0\right)=\operatorname{S}\!\left(\mathit{x}\right)\)

      Local hypothesis 1 of this reusable inference.

    2. \(\forall\,\mathit{x}\;\left(\left(\mathit{x}+0\right)=\mathit{x}\right)\)

      Use named theory axiom AZ.

    3. \(\left(\forall\,\mathit{x}\;\left(\left(\mathit{x}+0\right)=\mathit{x}\right)\Rightarrow \left(\mathit{x}+0\right)=\mathit{x}\right)\)

      Use the capture-avoiding universal instantiation scheme.

    4. \(\left(\mathit{x}+0\right)=\mathit{x}\)

      Apply modus ponens to local lines 2 and 3.

    5. \(\left(\mathit{x}+0\right)=\left(\mathit{x}+0\right)\)

      An expression equals itself.

    6. \(\left(\left(\mathit{x}+0\right)=\mathit{x}\Rightarrow \left(\left(\mathit{x}+0\right)=\left(\mathit{x}+0\right)\Rightarrow \mathit{x}=\left(\mathit{x}+0\right)\right)\right)\)

      Substitute equals for equals in the stated formula template.

    7. \(\left(\left(\mathit{x}+0\right)=\left(\mathit{x}+0\right)\Rightarrow \mathit{x}=\left(\mathit{x}+0\right)\right)\)

      Apply modus ponens to local lines 4 and 6.

    8. \(\mathit{x}=\left(\mathit{x}+0\right)\)

      Apply modus ponens to local lines 5 and 7.

    9. \(\operatorname{S}\!\left(\mathit{x}\right)=\operatorname{S}\!\left(\mathit{x}\right)\)

      An expression equals itself.

    10. \(\left(\mathit{x}=\left(\mathit{x}+0\right)\Rightarrow \left(\operatorname{S}\!\left(\mathit{x}\right)=\operatorname{S}\!\left(\mathit{x}\right)\Rightarrow \operatorname{S}\!\left(\mathit{x}\right)=\operatorname{S}\!\left(\left(\mathit{x}+0\right)\right)\right)\right)\)

      Substitute equals for equals in the stated formula template.

    11. \(\left(\operatorname{S}\!\left(\mathit{x}\right)=\operatorname{S}\!\left(\mathit{x}\right)\Rightarrow \operatorname{S}\!\left(\mathit{x}\right)=\operatorname{S}\!\left(\left(\mathit{x}+0\right)\right)\right)\)

      Apply modus ponens to local lines 8 and 10.

    12. \(\operatorname{S}\!\left(\mathit{x}\right)=\operatorname{S}\!\left(\left(\mathit{x}+0\right)\right)\)

      Apply modus ponens to local lines 9 and 11.

    13. \(\left(\operatorname{S}\!\left(\mathit{x}\right)=\operatorname{S}\!\left(\left(\mathit{x}+0\right)\right)\Rightarrow \left(\left(\operatorname{S}\!\left(\mathit{x}\right)+0\right)=\operatorname{S}\!\left(\mathit{x}\right)\Rightarrow \left(\operatorname{S}\!\left(\mathit{x}\right)+0\right)=\operatorname{S}\!\left(\left(\mathit{x}+0\right)\right)\right)\right)\)

      Substitute equals for equals in the stated formula template.

    14. \(\left(\left(\operatorname{S}\!\left(\mathit{x}\right)+0\right)=\operatorname{S}\!\left(\mathit{x}\right)\Rightarrow \left(\operatorname{S}\!\left(\mathit{x}\right)+0\right)=\operatorname{S}\!\left(\left(\mathit{x}+0\right)\right)\right)\)

      Apply modus ponens to local lines 12 and 13.

    15. \(\left(\operatorname{S}\!\left(\mathit{x}\right)+0\right)=\operatorname{S}\!\left(\left(\mathit{x}+0\right)\right)\)

      Apply modus ponens to local lines 1 and 14.

The first local-compaction audit hit its declared 512 MiB RSS ceiling while retaining both inventory graphs. Its exact source freeze, plan and log are preserved. That version failed to checkpoint elapsed time/peak RSS on its exception, so both remain unknown. Attempt 2 changed only graph lifetime and exception checkpoints, retaining the same 180-second and 512 MiB ceilings, and passed. No native rejection or theorem-search failure is inferred from this preparation unknown.

Unique native producer computations total 673.554 s / 90,808,412,227 microsteps across 38 queries. Four accepted byte-identical pilot requests are credited as historical admission, not repeated fresh cost. The original zero-left theorem’s preparation and 92-query certification history remains in its separate complete ledger, and is not treated as free. Native pilot replay and later admissions overlapped on the shared host; component sums are not a single invented end-to-end wall time. Manual infrastructure development and the first failed compaction-audit time are unmeasured.

The setup/native checker cost motivates the proved full-grammar pruning: 2095 authored local definitions become 24 unique reachable definitions to admit. This measured structural reduction is useful; it is not evidence of RL progress or superiority over existing automated theorem provers.
